=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N foods, each containing exactly one of vitamins 1, 2, and 3.
Specifically, eating the i-th food gives you A_i units of vitamin V_i, and C_i calories.
Takahashi can choose any subset of these N foods as long as the total calorie consumption does not exceed X.
Find the maximum possible value of this: the minimum intake among vitamins 1, 2, and 3.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N X
V_1 A_1 C_1
V_2 A_2 C_2
\vdots
V_N A_N C_N

Output

Print the maximum possible value of "the minimum intake among vitamins 1, 2, and 3" when the total calories consumed is at most X.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 5000
- 1 \leq X \leq 5000
- 1 \leq V_i \leq 3
- 1 \leq A_i \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq C_i \leq X
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 25
1 8 5
2 3 5
2 7 10
3 2 5
3 3 10

Sample Output 1

3

Each food provides the following if eaten:

- 1st food: 8 units of vitamin 1, and 5 calories
- 2nd food: 3 units of vitamin 2, and 5 calories
- 3rd food: 7 units of vitamin 2, and 10 calories
- 4th food: 2 units of vitamin 3, and 5 calories
- 5th food: 3 units of vitamin 3, and 10 calories

Eating the 1st, 2nd, 4th, and 5th foods gives 8 units of vitamin 1, 3 units of vitamin 2, 5 units of vitamin 3, and 25 calories.
In this case, the minimum among the three vitamin intakes is 3 (vitamin 2).
It is impossible to get 4 or more units of each vitamin without exceeding 25 calories, so the answer is 3.

Sample Input 2

2 5000
1 200000 1
2 200000 1

Sample Output 2

0

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Knapsack implementation:

def knapsack(items, X):
    dp = [0] * (X + 1)
    for w, v in items:
        # local variables for speed
        d = dp
        # for c in range(X, w - 1, -1):
        #   nv = d[c-w] + v
        #   if nv > d[c]: d[c] = nv
        for c in range(X, w - 1, -1):
            val = d[c - w] + v
            if val > d[c]:
                d[c] = val
    return dp

This is okay. But 25M loops with Python if inside. Could optimize by using local d, range. Maybe use for c in range(X, w-1, -1): if d[c-w] + v > d[c]: d[c] = d[c-w]+v. This computes d[c-w] twice if update. Better compute val. Good.

Could use list comprehension? Not for 0/1 knapsack due to dependency. Could use arrays from module array? List faster. Could use `for c in range(X, w - 1, -1):` with local `dp_local = dp`. Good.

Maybe if C_i large, loops fewer. Worst C_i=1 all items, 25M. 25M Python loops okay. But AtCoder Python time maybe 2 sec? Need maybe optimize further. Let's consider alternative algorithms.

Let's draft code:

import sys
from bisect import bisect_left

def knapsack(items, X):
    dp = [0] * (X + 1)
    for w, v in items:
        # local references
        d = dp
        # w is calorie, v is amount
        for c in range(X, w - 1, -1):
            val = d[c - w] + v
            if val > d[c]:
                d[c] = val
    return dp

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    it = iter(data)
    N = next(it); X = next(it)
    groups = [[], [], []]
    for _ in range(N):
        V = next(it); A = next(it); C = next(it)
        groups[V-1].append((C, A))
    if not groups[0] or not groups[1] or not groups[2]:
        print(0)
        return
    dps = [knapsack(g, X) for g in groups]
    high = min(dp[X] for dp in dps)
    # high should be > 0
    def feasible(t):
        total = 0
        for dp in dps:
            total += bisect_left(dp, t)
            if total > X:
                return False
        return True
    low = 0
    while low < high:
        mid = (low + high + 1) // 2
        if feasible(mid):
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid - 1
    print(low)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's write final code with comments maybe:

import sys
from bisect import bisect_left

def build_dp(items, limit):
    dp = [0] * (limit + 1)
    for cost, amount in items:
        for c in range(limit, cost - 1, -1):
            cand = dp[c - cost] + amount
            if cand > dp[c]:
                dp[c] = cand
    return dp

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, X = data[0], data[1]
    groups = [[], [], []]
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        v = data[idx]; a = data[idx+1]; c = data[idx+2]; idx += 3
        groups[v-1].append((c, a))
    if not groups[0] or not groups[1] or not groups[2]:
        print(0)
        return
    dps = [build_dp(g, X) for g in groups]
    high = min(dp[X] for dp in dps)
    def ok(t):
        need = 0
        for dp in dps:
            need += bisect_left(dp, t)
            if need > X:
                return False
        return True
    low = 0
    while low < high:
        mid = (low + high + 1) // 2
        if ok(mid):
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid - 1
    print(low)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

def build_dp(items, limit):
    dp = [0] * (limit + 1)
    for cost, amount in items:
        for c in range(limit, cost - 1, -1):
            if dp[c - cost] + amount > dp[c]:

    return dp

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "dp" list values maybe not sorted if using at-most DP but we can avoid relying on sorted by computing exact DP and prefix max. Should we switch to exact DP to be safer? Let's evaluate performance. Exact DP:

def build_dp(items, limit):
    neg = -10**30
    dp = [neg]*(limit+1)
    dp[0]=0
    for cost, amount in items:
        for c in range(limit, cost-1, -1):
            if dp[c-cost] != neg:
                val = dp[c-cost] + amount
                if val > dp[c]:
                    dp[c] = val
    # prefix max
    best = 0
    for i in range(limit+1):
        if dp[i] > best:
            best = dp[i]
        else:
            dp[i] = best
    return dp

This has extra if per loop and prefix max. At-most DP faster and correct. We'll keep at-most.

But let's double-check at-most DP with dp initialized 0 and descending update for 0/1 knapsack "max value with capacity at most c" is indeed standard. Some implementations initialize dp to 0 and do `for c in range(W, w-1, -1): dp[c] = max(dp[c], dp[c-w]+v)`. Yes, this computes max value with capacity exactly? Let's recall: If dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c, then dp[c] is nondecreasing. If dp[c] is max value with capacity exactly c, you'd initialize dp[0]=0 and -inf, and not prefix max. But many tutorials use dp[0]=0 and dp[c]=0 for all c and descending update to compute max value for capacity c (at most). Let's verify with a case where exact vs at-most differ: item weight 3 value 5, W=2. At-most dp[2]=0, exact dp[2]=-inf. Both okay for f(T) because T>0. For W=3, dp[3]=5. Good. Another case: item1 weight 2 value 10, item2 weight 3 value 1, W=4. At-most dp[4] after item1: dp[2..4]=10. After item2 descending: c=4: dp[4]=max(10, dp[1]+1=1)=10; c=3: dp[3]=max(10, dp[0]+1=1)=10. dp[4]=10. Exact dp[4] would be max subset weight exactly4? item1+item2 weight5 >4, none, -inf. But for capacity at most 4, max value 10. Good. f(10)=2. Correct. If we used exact and prefix max, f(10)=2. At-most directly gives dp[2]=10. Good.

Now, let's think about if at-most DP with dp initialized 0 and descending update can accidentally allow using an item multiple times? Descending prevents. Example item weight 1 value 1, W=3. Process: c=3: dp[3]=max(0, dp[2]+1). dp[2] initially 0, so dp[3]=1. c=2: dp[2]=max(0, dp[1]+1=1) ->1. c=1: dp[1]=1. Final dp[3]=1, not 3. Good. If ascending, would allow multiple. Descending correct.

Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

Let's maybe prove with induction one more time in case of a subtle issue: At-most DP update uses `dp[c] = max(old dp[c], old dp[c-w]+v)`. But `old dp[c-w]` is max value with capacity at most c-w. Suppose old dp[c-w] subset has weight w' <= c-w. Adding new item gives weight w'+w <= c. So candidate valid. Any optimal subset for new items either excludes new item -> old dp[c], or includes new item -> v + subset of old items with weight <= c-w. So update correct. Thus final dp[c] is max value with capacity at most c. Since capacity sets nested, dp[c] nondecreasing. So sorted. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
:
```python
import sys
from bisect import bisect_left


def build_dp(items, limit):
    dp = [0] * (limit + 1)
    for cost, amount in items:
        for c in range(limit, cost - 1, -1):
            val = dp[c - cost] + amount
            if val > dp[c]:
                dp[c] = val
    return dp


def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N, X = data[0], data[1]
    groups = [[], [], []]

    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        v = data[idx]
        a = data[idx + 1]
        c = data[idx + 2]
        idx += 3
        groups[v - 1].append((c, a))

    # If one vitamin does not appear at all, the minimum intake is always 0.
    if not groups[0] or not groups[1] or not groups[2]:
        print(0)
        return

    dps = [build_dp(g, X) for g in groups]

    # Upper bound: no vitamin can exceed its maximum obtainable amount with X calories.
    high = min(dp[X] for dp in dps)

    def feasible(t):
        need = 0
        for dp in dps:
            need += bisect_left(dp, t)
            if need > X:
                return False
        return True

    low = 0
    while low < high:
        mid = (low + high + 1) // 2
        if feasible(mid):
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid - 1

    print(low)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
